4016e302	<p>\nThe HTTP Server can be configured with many different authentication\ndirectives. The authentication is hierarchical in that any URI can\nbe given an authentication directive in order to override a parent URI\nauthentication directive.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>URI</b> must begin with <code>/</code> to refer to the filesystem.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe different <b>AuthType</b> values offer various levels of security.\nFrom the least to most secure:\n</p>\n<table>\n<tr>\n<td><b>None</b><br/>no authentication necessary</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><b>Basic</b><br/>encodes passwords using Base64</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><b>Digest</b><br/>encodes passwords using MD5</td>\n</tr>\n\n<tr>\n<td>\n<b>SSL</b><br/>\npage can only be accessed over SSL (no password)\n</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>\n<b>SSL/Basic</b><br/>\npage can only be accessed over SSL (encodes passwords using Base64)\n</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>\n<b>SSL/Digest</b><br/>\npage can only be accessed over SSL (encodes passwords using MD5)\n</td>\n</tr>\n\n</table>\n<p>\nWhen changing the parameters of <b>Digest</b> or <b>SSL/Digest</b>\nauthentication, it is often best to close and reopen the browser to ensure\nthat the it does not attempt to use cached authentication information.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote that <b>SSL</b> by itself does not require a password but all data\ntransferred to and from the HTTP Server is encrypted.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThere is no real reason to create an authentication directive using\n<b>None</b> unless you want to override a parent directive that uses some\nother <b>AuthType</b>.\n</p>\n<p>\nMultiple users can be configured within a single authentication directive.\n</p>\n

style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Max Timeout</b> value specifies the maximum amount of time to\nwait for a request from a client.\n</p>\n<p>\nAlong with the <b>Max Bytes</b> value,\nit is used to help prevent Denial of Service (DoS) attacks against\nthe HTTP Server.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_max_bytes" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Max Bytes</b> value specifies the maximum number of bytes\nallowed in a client request.\n</p>\n<p>\nAlong with the <b>Max Timeout</b> value,\nit is used to help prevent Denial of Service (DoS) attacks against\nthe HTTP Server.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_logging_state" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Enable</b> the <b>Logging State</b> to record recent HTTP requests\nreceived.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Disable</b> the <b>Logging State</b> to turn off the recording\nand free any allocated memory used by the log.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_max_log_entries" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe HTTP Log is a scrolling log in that only the last\n<b>Max Log Entries</b> lines are cached and viewable.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_log_format" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Log Format Directives</b><br/>\n<table>\n<tr>\n<td width="20%">%a</td>\n<td>remote IP address (could be a proxy)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%b</td>\n<td>bytes sent excluding headers</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%B</td>\n<td>bytes sent excluding headers (0 = '-')</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%h</td>\n<td>remote host (same as '%a')</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%{h}i</td>\n<td>header contents from request (h = header string)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%m</td>\n<td>request method</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%p</td>\n<td>ephemeral local port value used for request</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%q</td>\n<td>query string (prepend with '?' or empty '-')</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%t</td>\n<td>timestamp HH:MM:SS (same as Apache '%(%H:%M:%S)t' or '%(%T)t')</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%u</td>\n<td>remote user (could be bogus for 401 status)</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%U</td>\n<td>URL path info</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%r</td>\n<td>first line of request (same as '%m %U%q &lt;version&gt;')</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>%s</td>\n<td>return status</td>\n</tr>\n</table>\n</p>\n<p>\nThe max length for each directive is 64 bytes.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe exception is '%r' where each element is limited to 64 bytes\n(i.e. method, URL path info, and query string).\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_authentication_timeout" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Authentication Timeout</b> applies if the selected\nauthentication type is either <b>Digest</b> or <b>SSL/Digest</b>.\nAfter this period of inactivity, the client must authenticate again.\n</p>\n</div>\n

40172275	<p>Enabling <b>Banner</b> will force the banner page to be\nprinted even if the incoming print job does not specify to do so.</p>\n\n<p>Enabling <b>Binary</b> will pass the entire file to the printer without\nremoving any characters.\nDisabled, only valid ascii and control characters are passed; all\nothers are stripped. Valid control characters include the tab, linefeed,\nformfeed, backspace, and newline.</p>\n\n<p>Enabling <b>Formfeeds</b> will force a formfeed to be sent to the printer at\nthe end of each print job.</p>\n\n<p>Enabling <b>Convert Newlines</b> will convert single newlines and single\ncarriage returns into DOS style carriage return + linefeed line endings; if\ncarriage return and linefeed characters are already in the correct DOS\nline-ending order, they will remain unchanged.</p>\n\n<p>To send a Start Of Job (<b>SOJ</b>) or End Of Job (<b>EOJ</b>) string\nto the printer, enter the appropriate string.\nThe SOJ and EOJ strings are limited to 100 characters each\n(after possible conversion to binary).</p>\n\n<p>The SOJ and EOF strings can be entered in <b>Text</b> or <b>Binary</b>\nform. The Binary form allows square\nbraces <code>[ ]</code> to enclose one or more character designations separated by\ncommas. Use straight decimal numbers up to 255 or hexadecimal numbers prefixed\nwith 0x up to 0xFF within the square braces.\nTo specify an open brace in binary mode, use two in a row. Example (in\nBinary mode): <code>AB[255,0xFF]C[[D]</code> Results in a string containing\nbinary values where the dots appear: <code>AB&#183;&#183;C[D]</code></p>\n\n<p>A <b>Queue Name</b> may not contain white space.</p>\n

40178c61	<p>\nThis page is used to configure a network link using PPP\nover a serial line.\nNote that this device acts as the server\nside of the PPP link.\nThis device can force authentication\nand is able to assign an IP Address to the peer.\nOnce the PPP interface is up, IP packets are routed to\nand from the Ethernet and PPP interfaces.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Local IP Address</b> is the IP Address that will be\nassigned to the PPP interface on the device.\nIt may be entered alone, in CIDR form, or with an explicit mask:<br />\n192.168.1.1 (default mask)<br />\n192.168.1.1/24 (CIDR)<br />\n192.168.1.1 255.255.255.0 (explicit mask)<br />\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Peer IP Address</b> is the IP Address that will be assigned to\nthe peer if asked during negotiation.\n</p>\n<p>\n\nThere are four different authentication schemes supported\nby this device. <b>None</b> (no authentication),\nthe Password Authentication Protocol (<b>PAP</b>),\nthe Challenge-Handshake Authentication Protocol (<b>CHAP</b>),\nthe Microsoft Challenge-Handshake Authentication Protocol (<b>MS-CHAP</b>),\nand the MS-CHAP Version 2 (<b>MS-CHAPV2</b>).\n\n\n\n\n\n\n</p>\n<p>\n\nThe <b>Auth Username</b> and <b>Auth Password</b> are the\ncredentials used by the <b>PAP</b>, <b>CHAP</b>, <b>MS-CHAP</b>,\nand <b>MS-CHAPV2</b>\nauthentication protocols during link negotiation. If\nauthentication is to be used on the PPP interface, the peer\nmust be configured to use this username and password.\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n</p>\n

40179c9c	<p>\nAn RDF Site Summary (RSS) syndication feed is served by the HTTP Server.\nThis feed contains up-to-date information regarding the configuration\nchanges that occur on the device.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSpecifying the RSS Feed to be <b>Persistent</b> results in the data\nbeing stored on the filesystem. The file used is "<code>/cfg_log.txt</code>".\nThis allows feed data to be available across reboots (or until the\nfactory defaults are set).\n</p>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n<p>\nEach RSS Feed entry is prefixed with a timestamp as follows:\n"<code>[BC:HH:MM:SS]</code>". "<code>BC</code>" is the Boot\nCycle value. This value is the number of times the device has been\nrebooted since the factory defaults were last loaded. The resulting\n"<code>HH:MM:SS</code>" is the time since the device booted up. This\nsomewhat cryptic scheme is used because no Real Time Clock is available.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>\nThe RSS Feed is a scrolling feed in that only the last <b>Max Entries</b>\nentries are cached and viewable.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSimply register the RSS Feed within\nyour favorite RSS aggregator and you will automatically be notified of\nany configuration changes that occur.\n</p>\n

4017f85e	<p>\nThis page contains lower level TCP Network Stack specific\nconfiguration items.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Send RSTs</b> boolean is used to turn on/off sending of\nTCP RST messages.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Ack Limit</b> specifies how many packets must be received before an\nACK is forced. If there is a large amount of data to acknowledge, an ACK will\nbe forced before this.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf the sender TCP implementation waits for an ACK before sending more data\neven though the window is open, setting <b>Ack Limit</b> to "1" packet will\nimprove performance by forcing immediate acknowledgements.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Send Data</b> selection governs when data may be sent into the network.\nThe Standard implementation waits for an ACK before sending a packet less than\nthe maximum length.\nSelect Expedited to send data whenever the window allows it.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Max Retrans</b> specifies the maximum number of retransmissions of a\npacket that will be attempted before failing.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Max Retrans Syn/Ack</b> specifies the maximum number of retransmissions of a\nSYN that will be attempted before failing.\nIt is lower than "Max Retrans" to thwart denial-of-service attacks.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Max Timeout</b> specifies the maximum time between retransmissions.\n</p>\n

401851e4	<div id="help_base">\n<p>\n<b>Tunnel Accept Mode</b> controls how a tunnel behaves when a\nconnection attempt originates from the network.\n</p>\n\n<p id="help_base_ssh_Display">\nFor more information on <b>Protocol</b> SSH, see the\n<a href="javascript:void(0)"\nonclick="             var jsExists = window.sshJS;             return ajax_AddToHistory('SshServerAuthUsersPage', [true, (!jsExists ? 'sshJS' : '')]);         "\n\n\n\n>SSH Server: Authorized Users</a> page.\n</p>\n\n\n<p id="help_base_ssl_Display">\nFor more information on <b>Protocol</b> SSL, see the\n<a href="javascript:void(0)"\nonclick="             var jsExists = window.sslJS;             return ajax_AddToHistory('SslCertPage', [true, (!jsExists ? 'sslJS' : '')]);         "\n\n\n\n>SSL</a> page.\n</p>\n\n</div>\n<div id="help_mode" style="display:none">\n<p>A Tunnel in Accept Mode can be started in a number of ways:</p>\n<p><b>Disabled:</b> never started.</p>\n<p><b>Always:</b> always started.</p>\n<p>\n<b>Any Character:</b> started when any character is read on the\nSerial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Start Character:</b> started when the Start Character is read\non the Serial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Modem Control Asserted:</b> started when the Modem Control pin\nis asserted on the Serial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Modem Emulation:</b> behavior is governed by the ATS0 or\nIncoming Connection setting.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_start_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWhen the <b>Start Character</b> is received on the Serial Line,\nit enables the tunnel to listen for a network connection.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Start Character</b> may be designated as a single printable\ncharacter or as a control character.\n</p>\n<p>\nControl characters may be input in any of the following forms:<br />\n&lt;control&gt;J or<br />\n0xA (hexadecimal) or<br />\n\10 (decimal).\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_flush_start_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nEnabling <b>Flush Start Character</b> prevents forwarding of a start\ncharacter from the Line into the network.\n</p>\n<p>\nDisabling <b>Flush Start Character</b> allows forwarding of a start\ncharacter from the Line into the network.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_local_port" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Local Port</b> value can be overridden.\nBy default, it is 10001 for Tunnel 1, 10002 for Tunnel 2, and so on.\n</p>\n<p>\nBlank the display field to restore the default.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_protocol" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Protocol</b> used on the connection can be one of\n\nSSH,\n\n\nSSL,\n\nTelnet, TCP, or TCP with AES.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nIf security is a concern it is highly recommended that SSH be used.\nWhen using SSH, both the SSH Server Host Keys\nand SSH Server Authorized Users must be configured.\n</p>\n\n</div>\n<div id="help_keep_alive" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>TCP Keep Alive</b> timer specifies how often to probe the remote\nhost in order to keep the TCP connection up during idle transfer periods.\n</p>\n<p>\nEnter 0 to disable.\n</p>\n<p>\nBlank the display field to restore the default.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_aes" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThere are two Advanced Encryption Standard (AES) Encryption\nKeys used for Accept Mode Tunneling.\nOne Key is used for encrypting outgoing data and\nthe other Key is used for decrypting incoming data.\n</p>\n<p>\nThese AES Keys are 16, 24, or 32 bytes in length.\nEach key can be entered in <b>Text</b> or <b>Hexadecimal</b> form.\nKeys are stored and exchanged in <b>Hexadecimal</b> form only.\n</p>\n</p>\n<b>Text</b> form is a simple string of up to 32 ASCII characters.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Hexadecimal</b> form takes up to 32 byte specifications\nseparated by spaces. A byte specification comprises two nibble\nspecifications with no intervening space. A nibble specification\nis a single digit from 0 to 9 or from "a" to "f" (representing\n10 through 15).<br />\nExample Hexadecimal key: <br />\n<code>12 34 56 78 9a bc de f0 01 02 03 04 05 06 07 08</code>\n</p>\n<p>\nTo <b>remove</b> a key, delete &lt;Configured&gt; in the display.\n</p>\n<p>\nNote that the Keys are <b>shared secret keys</b> so they must be\nknown by both sides of the connection and kept secret.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis device also supports SSH using AES Encryption as an alternative\nto secure tunneling. It is recommended that SSH\nbe used because it does not require configuring shared secret keys and\nis a more secure standards based protocol.\n</p>\n\n</div>\n<div id="help_flush_serial" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Flush Serial</b> applies at the time when a connection is accepted\nfrom the network.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, any buffered characters from the Serial Line will be\ndiscarded when a connection is accepted.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b>, any characters received on the Serial Line will be\nbuffered and sent after a connection is accepted.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_block_serial" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Block Serial</b> may be enabled for debugging purposes.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, incoming characters from the Serial Line will NOT be\nforwarded to the network. Instead, they will be buffered and will\neventually flow off the Serial Line if hardware or software\nflow control is configured.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b> (the normal setting), incoming characters from the\nSerial Line are sent on into the network. Any buffered characters are\nsent first.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_block_network" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Block Network</b> may be enabled for debugging purposes.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, incoming characters from the network will NOT be\nforwarded to the Serial Line. Instead, they will be buffered and will\neventually flow off the network side.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b> (the normal setting), incoming characters from the\nnetwork are sent on into the Serial Line. Any buffered characters are\nsent first.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_password" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Password</b> can be up to 31 characters in length and must\ncontain only alphanumeric characters and punctuation.\nWhen set, clients must send the correct password string to the unit\nwithin 30 seconds from opening network connection in order to enable\ndata transmission.\nThe password sent to the unit must be terminated with one of the\nfollowing:\n<ul>\n<li>0A (Line Feed)</li>\n<li>00 (Null)</li>\n<li>0D 0A (Carriage Return / Line Feed)</li>\n<li>0D 00 (Carriage Return / Null)</li>\n</ul>\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Prompt for Password</b> is set to Enabled,\nthe user will be prompted for the password upon connection.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_email_connect" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Email on Connect</b> comes into play when an accept mode tunnel is\nestablished. If an Email profile number has been selected,\nan email notification based on that profile will then be sent.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_email_disconnect" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Email on Disconnect</b> comes into play when an accept mode tunnel is\ndisconnected. If an Email profile number has been selected,\nan email notification based on that profile will then be sent.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_cp_output_group" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>CP Output Group</b> identifies a CP Group whose value should\nchange when a connection is established and dropped.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Connection Value</b> specifies the value to set the CP Group\nto when a connection is established.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Disconnection Value</b> specifies the value to set the CP Group\nto when the connection is closed.\n</p>\n<p>\nTo display "Connection Value" and "Disconnection Value", first enter a\n"CP Output Group", then click outside that field.\n</p>\n</div>\n

never started.</p>\n<p><b>Always:</b> always started.</p>\n<p>\n<b>Any Character:</b> started when any character is read on the\nSerial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Start Character:</b> started when the Start Character is read\non the Serial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Modem Control Asserted:</b> started when the Modem Control pin\nis asserted on the Serial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Modem Emulation:</b> started by an ATD command.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_start_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWhen the <b>Start Character</b> is received on the Serial Line,\nit connects the tunnel.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Start Character</b> may be designated as a single printable\ncharacter or as a control character.\n</p>\n<p>\nControl characters may be input in any of the following forms:<br />\n&lt;control&gt;J or<br />\n0xA (hexadecimal) or<br />\n\10 (decimal).\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_flush_start_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nEnabling <b>Flush Start Character</b> prevents forwarding of a start\ncharacter from the Line into the network.\n</p>\n<p>\nDisabling <b>Flush Start Character</b> allows forwarding of a start\ncharacter from the Line into the network.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_host_summary" style="display:none">\n<p>\nAt least one <b>Host</b> is required to enable Connect Mode.\n</p>\n<p>\nIt contains the information necessary to connect to that host.\n</p>\n<p>\nClick on the displayed information to expand it for editing.\n</p>\n\n<div id="help_host_summary_up" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe button <img src="/config/up.gif"/>\n<b>promotes</b> the selected host,\nexchanging its place with the host above it,\nto adjust the order of the defined hosts.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n</div>\n<div id="help_host_address" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Host Address</b> is required to enable Connect Mode.\n</p>\n<p>\nIt designates the address of the remote host to connect to.\n</p>\n<p>\nEither a DNS address or an IP address may be provided.\n</p>\n\n<div id="help_host_address_up" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe button <img src="/config/up.gif"/>\n<b>promotes</b> the selected host,\nexchanging its place with the host above it,\nto adjust the order of the defined hosts.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n</div>\n<div id="help_host_port" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Host Port</b> is required to enable Connect Mode.\n</p>\n<p>\nIt designates the TCP or UDP port on the remote host to connect to.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_local_port" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Local Port</b> is by default random but can be overridden.\n</p>\n<p>\nBlank the field to restore the random default.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_protocol" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Protocol</b> used on the connection can be one of\n\nSSH,\n\n\nSSL,\n\nTelnet, TCP, TCP with AES, UDP, or UDP with AES.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nIf security is a concern it is highly recommended that SSH be used.\nWhen using SSH, both the SSH Server Host Keys\nand SSH Server Authorized Users must be configured.\n</p>\n\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_ssh_username" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>SSH Username</b> specifies the\nSSH Client User to use for an outgoing SSH connection.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n\n<div id="help_ssl_validate_certificate" style="display:none">\n<p>\nEnabling <b>Validate Certificate</b> requires the tunnel to verify the\nremote SSL server certificate when making a connection.\n</p>\n<p>\nDisabling <b>Validate Certificate</b> causes the tunnel to skip\nverification of the remote SSL server certificate.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_keep_alive" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>TCP Keep Alive</b> timer specifies how often to probe the remote\nhost in order to keep the TCP connection up during idle transfer periods.\n</p>\n<p>\nEnter 0 to disable.\n</p>\n<p>\nBlank the display field to restore the default.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_aes" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThere are two Advanced Encryption Standard (AES) Encryption\nKeys used for Connect Mode Tunneling.\nOne Key is used for encrypting outgoing data and\nthe other Key is used for decrypting incoming data.\n</p>\n<p>\nThese AES Keys are 16, 24, or 32 bytes in length.\nEach key can be entered in <b>Text</b> or <b>Hexadecimal</b> form.\nKeys are stored and exchanged in <b>Hexadecimal</b> form only.\n</p>\n</p>\n<b>Text</b> form is a simple string of up to 32 ASCII characters.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>Hexadecimal</b> form takes up to 32 byte specifications\nseparated by spaces. A byte specification comprises two nibble\nspecifications with no intervening space. A nibble specification\nis a single digit from 0 to 9 or from "a" to "f" (representing\n10 through 15).<br />\nExample Hexadecimal key: <br />\n<code>12 34 56 78 9a bc de f0 01 02 03 04 05 06 07 08</code>\n</p>\n<p>\nTo <b>remove</b> a key, delete &lt;Configured&gt; in the display.\n</p>\n<p>\nNote that the Keys are <b>shared secret keys</b> so they must be\nknown by both sides of the connection and kept secret.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis device also supports SSH using AES Encryption as an alternative\nto secure tunneling. It is recommended that SSH\nbe used because it does not require configuring shared secret keys and\nis a more secure standards based protocol.\n</p>\n\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_host_mode" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Host Mode</b> controls how multiple hosts shall be used\nin Connect Mode.\n</p>\n<p>\nWith <b>Sequential</b> selected, when it is time for the tunnel\nto connect, it will start with host 1 and attempt each host\nin sequence until a connection is accepted.\n</p>\n<p>\nWith <b>Simultaneous</b> selected, when it is time for the tunnel\nto connect, it will connect to all of the hosts that accept\na connection.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_reconnect_time" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Reconnect Time</b> specifies how long to wait in milliseconds\nbefore trying to reconnect to the remote host after a previous attempt\nfailed or the connection was closed.\n</p>\n<p>\nBlank the display field to restore the default.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_flush_serial" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Flush Serial</b> applies at the time when a connection is\nestablished to the network.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, any buffered characters from the Serial Line will be\ndiscarded when a connection is established.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b>, any characters received on the Serial Line will be\nbuffered and sent after a connection is established.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_block_serial" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Block Serial</b> may be enabled for debugging purposes.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, incoming characters from the Serial Line will NOT be\nforwarded to the network. Instead, they will be buffered and will\neventually flow off the Serial Line if hardware or software\nflow control is configured.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b> (the normal setting), incoming characters from the\nSerial Line are sent on into the network. Any buffered characters are\nsent first.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_block_network" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Block Network</b> may be enabled for debugging purposes.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Enabled</b>, incoming characters from the network will NOT be\nforwarded to the Serial Line. Instead, they will be buffered and will\neventually flow off the network side.\n</p>\n<p>\nIf <b>Disabled</b> (the normal setting), incoming characters from the\nnetwork are sent on into the Serial Line. Any buffered characters are\nsent first.\n</p>\n</div>\n\n<div id="help_email_connect" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Email on Connect</b> comes into play when a connect mode tunnel is\nestablished. If an Email profile number has been selected,\nan email notification based on that profile will then be sent.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_email_disconnect" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Email on Disconnect</b> comes into play when a connect mode tunnel is\ndisconnected. 4018bddc	<div id="help_base">\n<p>\nTunnel connections can be initiated and accepted using <b>Modem</b>\n"AT" commands incoming from the Serial Line.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_echo_pluses" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWith <b>Echo Pluses</b> enabled,\npluses will be echoed back during a "pause +++ pause" escape sequence\non the Serial Line.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_echo_commands" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWith <b>Echo Commands</b> enabled (ATE1),\ncharacters read on the Serial Line will be echoed while the Line is in\nModem Command Mode.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_verbose_response" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWith <b>Verbose Reponse</b> enabled (ATQ0),\nModem Response Codes are sent out on the Serial Line.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_response_type" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Response Type</b> selects either Text (ATV1) or Numeric (ATV0)\nrepresentation for the Modem Response Codes sent out on the\nSerial Line.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_error_unknown_commands" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWith <b>Error Unknown Commands</b> enabled (ATU0),\nERROR is returned on the Serial Line for unrecognized AT commands.\nOtherwise (ATU1) OK is returned for unrecognized AT commands.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_incoming_connection" style="display:none">\n<p>\n<b>Incoming Connection</b> requests may be disabled (ATS0=0),\nanswered automatically (ATS0=1), or\nanswered manually via the ATA command after an incoming RING (ATS0=2).\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_connect_string" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Connect String</b> is a customized string that is sent to the\nSerial Line with the CONNECT Modem Response Code.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_display_remote_ip" style="display:none">\n<p>\nWith <b>Display Remote IP</b> enabled,\nthe incoming RING sent on the Serial Line is followed by the IP address\nof the caller.\n</p>\n</div>\n

4018dc1e	<div id="help_base">\n<p>\nWhen Tunneling, instead of sending data on the network immediately\nafter being read on the Serial Line, the data can be <b>Packed</b>\n(queued) and sent in larger chunks.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_mode" style="display:none">\n<p>\nA Tunnel can be configured to use Packing <b>Mode</b> in the following\nways:\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Disable:</b> data not packed.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Timeout:</b> data sent after timeout occurs.\n</p>\n<p>\n<b>Send Character:</b> data sent when the Send Character is read on\nthe Serial Line.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_threshold" style="display:none">\n<p>\nIf the number of bytes of queued data reaches the <b>Threshold</b>,\nthe queued data will be sent on the network immediately.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_timeout" style="display:none">\n<p>\nIf the oldest byte of queued data has been waiting for <b>Timeout</b>\nmilliseconds, the queued data will be sent on the network immediately.\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_send_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nIf used, the <b>Send Character</b> is a single printable character\nor a control character that, when read on the Serial Line, forces the\nqueued data to be sent on the network immediately.\n</p>\n<p>\nControl characters may be input in any of the following forms:<br />\n&lt;control&gt;J or<br />\n0xA (hexadecimal) or<br />\n\10 (decimal).\n</p>\n</div>\n<div id="help_trailing_character" style="display:none">\n<p>\nThe <b>Trailing Character</b> is an optional single printable character\nor control character that is injected into the outgoing data stream\nright after the <b>Send Character</b>.\n</p>\n<p>\nControl characters may be input in any of the following forms:<br />\n&lt;control&gt;J or<br />\n0xA (hexadecimal) or<br />\n\10 (decimal).\n</p>\n<p>\nDisable the <b>Trailing Character</b> by blanking the field to set it to\n&lt;None&gt;.\n</p>\n</div>\n

4018f52b	<p>\nThis page allows you to manage the <b>Configurable Pin (CP) Groups</b>\non the device. CPs can be grouped together and based on their state,\ncan trigger an outside event like sending an Email message or starting\nthe CLI on a Serial Line. Only a Group that is enabled can be used.\n</p>\n<p>\nHere Groups can be created and deleted, enabled and disabled, CPs\nadded and removed, and the current value of the Group modified.\n</p>\n<p>\nCPs can be <b>add</b>ed to a Group at a specific <b>bit</b> position\nas an <b>Input</b> or <b>Output</b> and\nas positive logic or negative (<b>Assert Low</b>) logic.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe current <b>value</b> of the Group can be modified. This value is\nused to modify the specific bits where the CPs\ncurrently reside in the Group. For example, using a value of 5 would\nset the CPs at bits 0 and 2 and clear any other CPs. Using a value of\n0 would clear all the CPs in the group. Note that a CP can only be\nmodified if it is configured as <b>output</b>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe following groups are built-in.\nAdd the desired CP to the first (bit 0) position and enable to use:\n\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_RTS_CTS</b> is asserted while hardware flow control is set.\n\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_RS485_Select</b> is asserted while in any RS485 mode.\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_RS485_HDpx</b> is asserted while in half duplex\nRS485 mode.\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_RS485_TxEnable</b> is asserted when transmitting in\nhalf duplex RS485 mode.\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_Modem_Ctl_O</b> is asserted while a tunnel is active.\n<br/>\n<b>Line</b><i>X</i><b>_Modem_Ctl_In</b> controls Tunnel connections.\nThis group is only enabled automatically by selecting "Modem Control Asserted"\nin Tunnel Accept or Connect Modes\nor "Modem Control" in Tunnel Disconnect Mode.\n\n<br/>\n<b>Link_Status</b> is asserted while network Ethernet link is on.\n\n\n<br/>\n<b>Diagnostic_Mode</b> use two CPs to drive LEDs on the test board, it functions as diagnostic indicator.\n\n\n<br/>\n<b>Modbus_Ctl_In</b> and <b>Modbus_Ctl_Out</b> can be assigned CPs to read or\nwrite, respectively, via Unit Identifier 0xFF from Modbus TCP.\n\n</p>\n

4018fea6	<h2>CPM: CPs</h2>\n<div id="results"></div>\n<h3>Current Configuration</h3>\n<div id="cpmpinstable"></div>\n<h3>CP Status</h3>\n<div id="cpmstatustable"></div>\n
4018ff42	<p>\nThis page allows you to manage the <b>Configurable Pins (CP)</b> on the device.\nCPs can be grouped together and based on their state, can trigger an outside\nevent like sending an Email message or starting the CLI on a Serial Line.\n</p>\n<p>\nEach CP is associated with an external hardware pin and can be configured\nin either <b>input</b> or <b>output</b> mode.\nWhen a CP is configured as <b>output</b>,\nit can be toggled by setting the value.\nWhatever value is given, the first bit (bit 0) is used as the setting.\n<b>1</b> means asserted and <b>0</b> means de-asserted.\nAdditionally, the CP logic can be <b>inverted</b> so that assertion is low.\n</p>\n<p>\nA CP can be a member of multiple groups but can only be a member of one\nenabled group.\nNote that a CP can only be modified if all the groups it is a member of\nare disabled.\n</p>\n<p>\nThe <b>CP Status</b> chart shows the current status for an individual CP.\nA CP contains one bit of information and the <b>Value</b> shows the current\nvalue.\nThe <b>Level</b> row shows the voltage as '<code>+</code>' for high and\n'<code>-</code>' for low.\nThe <b>I/O</b> row shows input '<code>I</code>' or output '<code>O</code>'.\nAn '<code>I</code>' in the <b>Logic</b> row means the CP is inverted.\nLastly, a listing is shown of all groups the CP is a member of.\n</p>\n

4019b17e	<p>\nWhen Command Mode is enabled, the Command Line Interface (CLI)\nis attached to the Serial Line. Command Mode can be enabled\nin a number of ways:\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <b>Always</b> choice immediately enables Command Mode\nfor the Serial Line.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <b>Use Serial String</b> choice enables Command Mode\nwhen the Serial String is read on the Serial Line\nduring boot time.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>\nThe <b>Use CP Group</b> choice enables Command Mode\nbased on the status of a\n<a\nhref="javascript:void(0)"\nonclick="        var jsExists = window.cpmJS;        return ajax_AddToHistory(            'CpmGroupsPage', [true, (!jsExists ? This timer starts\nright after the Signon Message has been sent on the Serial\nLine.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <b>Serial String</b> is a string of bytes that must be\nread on the Serial Line during boot time in order to enable\nCommand Mode. It may contain a <b>time element</b> to\nspecify a required delay in milliseconds x, formed as {x}.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <b>Signon Message</b> is a string of bytes that is sent\non the Serial Line during boot time.\n</p>\n\n<p>\n<b>Binary</b> form is one or more byte values seperated by commas.\nEach byte value may be decimal or Hexadecimal.\nStart Hexadecimal values with 0x.\n</p>\n

401a2dc7	<p>\nThis page is used for importing system configuration\nfrom an XML file.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nImport <b>Configuration from External file</b> picks up all the settings\nfrom the external file.\nImport <b>Configuration from Filesystem</b> picks up settings from\nthe selected Groups, Lines and Instances.\nImport <b>Line(s) from single line Settings on the Filesystem</b> copies\nlines settings from an the input file containing only one Line instance\nto all of the selected Lines.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nWhen selecting a <b>Whole Groups to Import</b> item,\nall instances of that group will be imported.\nNotice that by default,\nall groups are checked except some pertaining to the\nnetwork configuration; this is so that import will not break\nyour network connectivity.\nYou may check or uncheck any group\nto include or omit that group from import.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSelection of <b>Lines to Import</b> filters instances to be imported in the\n\n\n\nline,\n\nlpd,\n\n\nppp,\n\nserial, tunnel, and terminal groups.\nThis affects both <b>Whole Groups to Import</b> and <b>Text List</b> selections.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nUse the <b>Text List</b> string to import specific instances of a group.\nThe textual format of this string is:\n</p>\n<pre>\n&lt;g&gt;:&lt;i&gt;;&lt;g&gt;:&lt;i&gt;;...\n</pre>\n<p>\nEach group name &lt;g&gt; is followed by a colon and the\ninstance value &lt;i&gt; and each &lt;g&gt;:&lt;i&gt;\nvalue is separated by a semi-colon. If a group has no\ninstance then only the group name &lt;g&gt; should be\nspecified.\n</p>\n
